Then she bethought herself of certain thoughts of comfort which had not failed to interject themselves into her heart, and withdrew with a little precipitation, alarmed by the inconsistency﻿—the insincerity of her feelings. “Get up, Freddy; you are not going away, except home to the colony, where you want to go,” she said. “Be good, all the same; for you know you must not trouble mamma. And make haste, and don’t be always calling for Nettie. Don’t you know you must do without Nettie some time? Jump up, and be a man.”

===Chinese===

当那初升的太阳红彤彤的光辉，照进妮蒂昨夜曾经向外张望的那扇窗子时，那一片冬日的天光照了进来，带着震荡人心的启示——照着的还不单是又一个早晨，而是一个新的世界。照例，妮蒂的心思总是拿有形有迹的东西表达出来的。她那张辗转未眠的床，在天色还几乎没有亮的时候，她便离开了；如今看她这副样子，一个外路人也许要当她是带着偏执狂那种一往不变的恒心，接着做她昨夜的活计。小弗雷迪在小床里坐起来，揉着眼睛，纳罕妮蒂究竟在忙什么——说实在的，随便什么人也都得纳罕。满眼是箱子抽屉，成堆的私人物件，她究竟要做什么？这一问她要回答，先得忍着痛才答得出；不过你若等得够久，妮蒂的目的自会分明。她身上还没有全然脱尽那副惶惶急急的神气——还没有从这一发现的激动里缓过劲来；她正拿一场意味深长的重新安置，把这一家的所有物通体重排一遍，来疏泄她那闲不下来的忙乱劲儿。她用飞快的手指，把那些直到如今还亲亲热热堆在一处、不分彼此的家当，一一分拣开来。多少年来头一遭，妮蒂动手把自己名下的东西，从那一总的家底里甄别出来；而且，也许为了把这一场割裂弄得柔和些，她又把那些往后不再归她照管的小衣柜，一件一件理出和谐的次序来。弗雷迪睁眼看见他自己那些独专属意的什物——那些他带着小孩子死攥不放的所有权、一一认得清的东西——正凄凄惶惶、孤孤零零地，被装进一只只属于它们自己的小箱子。这孩子不知道，趁他睡着的时候，会有什么可怕的判决判到了他头上。他瞪着那一双飞快而不容分说的手指，可怜的小胸腔里，呜咽一点一点升上来。那一场无声的风暴，随着他眼看那些熟识的物件一件接着一件过去，起伏着、涨起来；等到他那顶新的添头——衣柜里最新到手的一件——被端端正正、郑重其事地抚平了搁在最上头，弗雷迪的忍耐再也担不住了。他迸出一声长长的悲号，大声唤妮蒂来给他解开这个哑谜。他是不是要被打发出去了？是不是有什么神秘的刽子手、黑人，或是别的什么命运的可怕幻影，要来捉这牺牲品？弗雷迪这一声哀告，把那位已经退位的家庭主权者从活计里唤了起来。「要是打发我走，我偏不走！」弗雷迪嚷道。「我跑出去，再跑回来。你不走，我哪儿也不去，妮蒂。我要抓得死死的，你撵不开我；还有，哦，我要乖！——我要乖乖的！」素不惯于抚爱的妮蒂，这时走上前去，蓦地张开两臂，搂住她特别眷爱的小娃娃。她把脸颊贴上他的脸颊，天性里的柔情，小小地迸发了一场。「要被打发走的是我！」妮蒂嚷道，一时间顺着这天性的酸苦淌了出来。随即她记起那几缕不曾不来挤进她心头的宽慰之念，便有些急急地缩了回去——她心绪里这种前后不一、这种不真诚，把她自己都吓着了。「起来吧，弗雷迪；你不会被打发走，顶多是回家去、回殖民地去——那不是你巴望去的么，」她说。「照旧要乖；你知道的，不许麻烦妈妈。还要快些儿，别动不动就喊妮蒂。你不知道往后总有一天，不用妮蒂你也得过么？跳起来，做个男子汉。」

===Original===

“When I am a man, I shan’t want you,” said Freddy, getting up with reluctance; “but I can’t be a man now. And what am I to do with the buttons if you won’t help me? I shall not have buttons like those when I am a man.”

It was not in human nature to refrain from giving the little savage an admonitory shake. “That is all I am good for﻿—nothing but buttons!” said Nettie, with whimsical mortification. When they went down to breakfast, she sent the child before her, and came last instead of first, waiting till they were all assembled. Mrs. Fred watched her advent with apprehensive eyes. Thinking it over after her first triumph, it occurred to Mrs. Fred that the loss of Nettie would make a serious difference to her own comfort. Who was to take charge of the children, and conduct those vulgar affairs for which Susan’s feelings disqualified her? She did her best to decipher the pale face which appeared over the breakfast cups and saucers opposite. What did Nettie mean to do? Susan revolved the question in considerable panic, seeing but too clearly that the firm little hand no longer trembled, and that Nettie was absorbed by her own thoughts﻿—thoughts with which her present companions had but little to do. Mrs. Fred essayed another stroke.

===Chinese===

「等我做了男子汉，我就用不着你了，」弗雷迪老大不情愿地爬起来说；「可我现在还做不成男子汉。再说，你要是不帮我，这些扣子叫我怎么办呢？等我做了男子汉，我就不会有这样的扣子了。」

谁也熬不住，不给这个小野人警告似的一摇。「敢情我的用处全在这儿了——除了扣子，什么都不是！」妮蒂说，带着一种古里古怪的委屈。下楼吃早饭的时候，她打发孩子走在前头，自己却殿了后，不再抢先，直等到一家人都聚齐了。弗雷德太太提心吊胆，一双眼睛望着她的来临。头一场胜利之后细细一想，弗雷德太太忽然悟到，失了妮蒂，对她自己的安逸是个何等重大的亏空。往后谁来看管孩子们，谁来操办那些俗务——苏珊的心性是万万沾不得这些的？她竭力去辨认对面那副脸孔——从早饭的杯盘上沿露出来的、白白的脸。妮蒂到底打算怎样？苏珊怀着不小的心慌，把这个问题翻来覆去；她看得分明，也分明得过了头：那只坚定的小手不再颤抖了，妮蒂整个儿沉在她自己的心事里——那些心事，同眼前陪着她的人，是不大相干的。弗雷德太太又试着下了一着。

===Original===

“Perhaps I was hasty, Nettie, last night; but Richard, you know, poor fellow,” said Susan, “was not to be put off. It won’t make any difference between you and me, Nettie dear? We have always been so united, whatever has happened; and the children are so fond of you; and as for me,” said Mrs. Fred, putting back the strings of her cap, and pressing her handkerchief upon her eyes, “with my health, and after all I have gone through, how I could ever exist without you, I can’t tell; and Richard will be so pleased﻿—”

“I don’t want to hear anything about Richard, please,” said Nettie﻿—“not so far as I am concerned. I should have taken you out, and taken care of you, had you chosen me; but you can’t have two people, you know. One is enough for anybody. Never mind what we are talking about, Freddy. It is only your buttons﻿—nothing else. As long as you were my business, I should have scorned to complain,” said Nettie, with a little quiver of her lip. “Nothing would have made me forsake you, or leave you to yourself; but now you are somebody else’s business; and to speak of it making no difference, and Richard being pleased, and so forth, as if I had nothing else to do in the world, and wanted to go back to the colony! It is simply not my business any longer,” cried Nettie, rising impatiently from her chair﻿—“that is all that can be said. But I shan’t desert you till I deliver you over to my successor, Susan﻿—don’t fear.”

“Then you don’t feel any love for us, Nettie! It was only because you could not help it. Children, Nettie is going to leave us,” said Mrs. Fred, in a lamentable voice.

“Then who is to be instead of Nettie? Oh, look here﻿—I know﻿—it’s Chatham,” said the little girl.

“I hate Chatham,” said Freddy, with a little shriek. “I shall go where Nettie goes﻿—all my things are in my box. Nettie is going to take me; she loves me best of you all. I’ll kick Chatham if he touches me.”

===Chinese===

「昨儿晚上，兴许是我急躁了些，妮蒂；可是理查德，你知道，可怜的人儿，」苏珊说，「是推脱不得的。这总不会在咱们两个中间生分了吧，妮蒂亲爱的？不管出过什么事，咱们向来是那样同心合意的；孩子们又都那么疼你；至于我呢，」弗雷德太太说着，把帽带往后拢了拢，拿手帕按在眼睛上，「凭我这副身子，又经过我熬过的这一切，没有你我怎么活得成，我说不上来；理查德也要多高兴——」

「理查德的事，劳驾什么也不要讲给我听，」妮蒂说——「至少别对我本人讲。你当初若是拣中了我，我原会把你们带出去，把你们照管得好好的；可一个人不能要两个，你知道的。谁都一样，有一个尽够了。我们在这儿说的是什么，弗雷迪你别管。不过是你的扣子——没有别的。只要你们还是我的正经营生，我就根本不屑于叫苦，」妮蒂说着，嘴唇微微一颤。「什么也不能叫我撇下你们，把你们丢开不管；可如今你们是别人的正经营生了；偏偏还要说什么这没有两样、理查德要多高兴、等等，倒好像我在世上再没有别的事可做，倒巴望着回殖民地去似的！说穿了，这已经不再是我的事了，」妮蒂烦躁地从椅子上立起身来，嚷道，「话也只能说到这里。可是你们放心，苏珊，不到我把你们交割给我的后任手里，我总不会丢下你们——不用怕。」

「这么说，你对我们就半点儿疼爱也没有了，妮蒂！你先前不过是不得不罢了。孩子们哪，妮蒂要丢下我们去了，」弗雷德太太说，声气里一片哀鸣。

「那往后谁来顶妮蒂的缺呢？哦，瞧这儿——我知道了——是查塔姆，」那小姑娘说。

「我恨查塔姆，」弗雷迪尖叫了一声。「妮蒂上哪儿我跟她上哪儿——我的东西全在我那口箱子里。妮蒂要带我走；你们里头数她最疼我。查塔姆敢碰我，我就踢他。」

===Original===

“Why can’t someone tell Nettie she’s to go too?” said the eldest boy. “She’s most good of all. What does Nettie want to go away for? But I don’t mind; for we have to do what Nettie tells us, and nobody cares for Chatham,” cried the sweet child, making a triumphant somersault out of his chair. Nettie stood looking on, without attempting to stop the tumult which arose. She left them with their mother, after a few minutes, and went out to breathe the outside air, where at least there was quiet and freedom. To think as she went out into the red morning sunshine that her old life was over, made Nettie’s head swim with bewildering giddiness. She went up softly, like a creature in a dream, past St. Roque’s, where already the Christmas decorators had begun their pretty work﻿—that work which, several ages ago, being yesterday, Nettie had taken the children in to see. Of all things that had happened between that moment and this, perhaps the impulse of escaping out into the open air without anything to do, was one of the most miraculous. Insensibly Nettie’s footsteps quickened as she became aware of that extraordinary fact. The hour, the temperature, the customs of her life, were equally against such an indulgence. It was a comfort to recollect that, though everything else in the universe was altered, the family must still have some dinner, and that it was as easy to think while walking to the butcher’s as while idling and doing nothing. She went up, accordingly, towards Grange Lane, in a kind of wistful solitude, drifted apart from her former life, and not yet definitely attached to any other, feeling as though the few passengers she met must perceive in her face that her whole fortune was changed. It was hard for Nettie to realise that she could do absolutely nothing at this moment, and still harder for her to think that her fate lay undecided in Edward Rider’s hands. Though she had not a doubt of him, yet the mere fact that it was he who must take the first step was somewhat galling to the pride and temper of the little autocrat. Before she had reached the butcher, or even come near enough to recognise Lucy Wodehouse, where she stood at the garden gate, setting out for St. Roque’s, Nettie heard the headlong wheels of something approaching which had not yet come in sight. She wound herself up in a kind of nervous desperation for the encounter that was coming. No need to warn her who it was. Nobody but the doctor flying upon wings of haste and love could drive in that breakneck fashion down the respectable streets of Carlingford. Here he came sweeping round that corner at the Blue Boar, where Nettie herself had once mounted the drag, and plunged down Grange Lane in a maze of speed which confused horse, vehicle, and driver in one indistinct gleaming circle to the excited eyes of the spectator, who forced herself to go on, facing them with an exertion of all her powers, and strenuous resistance of the impulse to turn and escape. Why should Nettie escape?﻿—it must be decided one way or other. She held on dimly with rapid trembling steps. To her own agitated mind, Nettie, herself, left adrift and companionless, seemed the suitor. The only remnants of her natural force that remained to her united in the one resolution not to run away.

===Chinese===

「干吗没有人跟妮蒂说一声，叫她也一块儿去呢？」最大的男孩子说。「数她顶好。妮蒂干吗要走呢？不过我不在乎；反正我们得听妮蒂的话，谁也不把查塔姆放在心上，」那天真的孩子嚷着，一个得胜的筋斗翻出了椅子。妮蒂立在一旁看着，并不想去拦那阵哄然而起的闹腾。过了几分钟，她把他们留给母亲，自己走了出去，透一透外头的空气——那外头，至少还有清静，还有自由。一走进早晨红彤彤的日光里，一想到她的旧日子已经完了，妮蒂便头晕眼花，旋起一阵迷乱的晕眩。她轻悄悄地往上走，像梦里的一条生灵，走过圣罗克——那里，圣诞装点的人已经动手做起他们那好看的营生了；那营生，好几个世纪以前——也就是昨天——妮蒂还领着孩子们进去看过。从那一刻到这一刻之间发生的种种里头，或许顶近乎奇迹的一件，便是这一阵冲动：手头什么正事也没有，只管跑到露天里去。妮蒂一意识到这桩出奇的事，脚步便不知不觉地快了。论时辰，论天时，论她过日子的成规，都一概容不得这样的放纵。可有一件叫她宽心：纵然宇宙间的一切都变了，这一家人总还得吃一顿饭；而且走着上肉铺去的路上想心事，同闲着无所事事地想，一样地容易。于是她朝着格兰奇巷那头走去，怀着一种怅望的孤清：从旧日的生活里漂离了出来，却还没有确凿地傍上另一种；她只觉得迎面遇见的寥寥几个行人，必定从她脸上看得出来，她的整个命数都换了。妮蒂很难叫自己相信，此刻的她当真是什么也做不得；更难的是想到，她的命运还悬而未决，搁在爱德华·莱德的手心里。她对他并没有一丝疑心；可单单这一件——头一步必须由他先迈——落在那个小小霸主的自尊和性子上，就有些硌得难受。她还没走到肉铺，甚至还没走近到认得出站在园门口、正要动身往圣罗克去的露西·伍德豪斯，就听见一阵什么东西闯将过来的车轮声，那东西自己还没有照面。她把周身紧拧起来，怀着一种神经质的绝望，等着那正在逼近的相遇。不必谁来提点她，来的是谁。能赶着车、那样不要命地冲下卡林福德那些体面街道的，除了医生——那个展开急迫与爱情的双翼飞驰的人——再没有第二个。只见他从蓝猪旅馆的那个墙角兜将出来——妮蒂自己就曾在那里登上马车——一头扎下格兰奇巷，那速度纷乱迷人眼，把马、车、赶车的人搅成一团不分明、亮闪闪的圆环，在那看客激动不已的眼睛里旋转着；那看客强逼着自己往前走，倾出全副的力气迎着他们，死死摁住那转身逃开的冲动。妮蒂凭什么要逃？——这件事总得有个了断，不是这一头，便是那一头。她昏昏地撑着，脚步又急又颤。在她自己那颗震荡的心里，妮蒂——这个漂离无伴的人——倒像是来求亲的那一个。她天性里仅存的几星余力，全归并成了一个决心：不逃。

===Original===

It was well for the doctor that his little groom had the eyes and activity of a monkey, and knew the exact moment at which to dart forward and catch the reins which his master flung at him, almost without pausing in his perilous career. The doctor made a leap out of the drag, which was more like that of a mad adventurer than a man whose business it was to keep other people’s limbs in due repair. Before Nettie was aware that he had stopped, he was by her side.

“Dr. Edward,” she exclaimed, breathlessly, “hear me first! Now I am left unrestrained, but I am not without resources. Don’t think you are bound in honour to say anything over again. What may have gone before I forget now. I will not hold you to your word. You are not to have pity upon me!” cried Nettie, not well aware what she was saying. The doctor drew her arm into his; found out, sorely against her will, that she was trembling, and held her fast, not without a sympathetic tremor in the arm on which she was constrained to lean.

“But I hold you to yours!” said the doctor; “there has not been any obstacle between us for months but this; and now it is gone, do you think I will forget what you have said, Nettie? You told me it was impossible once﻿—”

“And you did not contradict me, Dr. Edward,” said the wilful creature, withdrawing her hand from his arm. “I can walk very well by myself, thank you. You did not contradict me! You were content to submit to what could not be helped. And so am I. An obstacle which is only removed by Richard Chatham,” said Nettie, with female cruelty, turning her eyes full and suddenly upon her unhappy lover, “does not count for much. I do not hold you to anything. We are both free.”

===Chinese===

也真亏得医生，他那小马童有猴子一般的眼睛和身手，又懂得那分毫不差的时刻：蹿上前去，接住主人几乎不停蹄地甩给他的缰绳。医生从车上一跃而下，那一跃，倒像个发狂的冒险家，不像个职分在身、专替旁人把肢体修理齐整的人。妮蒂还没觉出他已经停住，他已经到了她身边。

「爱德华医生，」她上气不接下气地嚷道，「先听我讲！如今我脱开了拘管，可我也并不是毫无退步。你别以为顾着信义，还得把那些话再说一遍不可。从前的事，此刻我都忘了。你的话，我不拿来捆你。你也不许来可怜我！」妮蒂嚷道，连自己在说什么也不大清楚。医生把她的手臂拢进自己的臂弯里；她极不愿意，他还是发觉了她在发抖，便紧紧地攥住她；那条她不得不倚靠的手臂里，也免不了一阵出于同情的颤动。

「可你的话，我要拿来捆你！」医生说。「这几个月以来，横在我们中间的障碍，除了这一桩，再没有别的；如今这一桩去了，你以为我会忘掉你说过的话么，妮蒂？你从前告诉过我，说那是不可能的——」

「可你当时并没有驳我，爱德华医生，」那任性的人儿说，把手从他的臂弯里抽了出来。「我自己走，走得很好，多谢你。你没有驳我！你安于屈从那无可如何的事。我也是。一个单靠理查德·查塔姆才除得掉的障碍，」妮蒂说着，带着女性那种狠心，把眼睛满满地、陡然地转过来看定她那位不幸的情人，「算不得多大分量。我不拿任何东西捆你。我们两个都是自由的。」

===Original===

What dismayed answer the doctor might have made to this heartless speech can never be known. He was so entirely taken aback that he paused, clearing his throat with but one amazed exclamation of her name; but before his astonishment and indignation had shaped itself into words, their interview was interrupted. An irregular patter of hasty little steps, and outcries of a childish voice behind, had not caught the attention of either in that moment of excitement; but just as Nettie delivered this cruel outbreak of feminine pride and self-assertion, the little pursuing figure made up to them, and plunged at her dress. Freddy, in primitive unconcern for anybody but himself, rushed head-foremost between these two at the critical instant. He made a clutch at Nettie with one hand, and with all the force of the other thrust away the astonished doctor. Freddy’s errand was of life or death.

“I shan’t go with anyone but Nettie,” cried the child, clinging to her dress. “I hate Chatham and everybody. I will jump into the sea and swim back again. I will never, never leave go of her, if you should cut my hands off. Nettie! Nettie!﻿—take me with you. Let me go where you are going! I will never be naughty any more! I will never, never go away till Nettie goes! I love Nettie best! Go away, all of you!” cried Freddy, in desperation, pushing off the doctor with hands and feet alike. “I will stay with Nettie. Nobody loves Nettie but me.”

===Chinese===

医生听了这句无情的话，会作出怎样一句心慌意乱的回答，世人永远无从知道了。他猝不及防到了十足的地步，怔在那里，喉咙里清了一清，只进出惊异的一声，唤的是她的名字；可他的惊骇与义愤还没来得及凝成言语，这一场相会就被打断了。方才那一阵乱踏的急促小步、和背后孩子嗓音的叫喊，在那激动的时刻，两个人都不曾留意；可正当妮蒂把这番女性的自尊与自恃、这狠心的一迸倾泻出来时，那小小的追赶的身影已经赶到他们跟前，一头扑上她的衣裳。弗雷迪浑然不管世上还有旁人，只顾自己，在那要紧关头，头朝前从这两个人当中直冲进来。他一只手一把抓住妮蒂，另一只手使出全身的力气，把那位愕然的医生推开。弗雷迪这趟来意，是性命交关的。

「除了妮蒂，我谁也不跟，」孩子攀着她的衣裳嚷道。「查塔姆我恨，人人都恨。我就跳下海去，游着再回来。你就是把我的手剁下来，我也永远、永远不放开她。妮蒂！妮蒂！——带我走吧。你上哪儿，让我也上哪儿！我再也不淘气了！妮蒂不走，我就永远、永远不走！我最爱妮蒂！你们全走开！」弗雷迪急得没了法子，手脚一齐用上，把医生往外推。「我要跟妮蒂待在一块儿。疼妮蒂的，除了我没有别人。」

===Original===

Nettie had no power left to resist this new assault. She dropped down on one knee beside the child, and clasped him to her in a passion of restrained tears and sobbing. The emotion which her pride would not permit her to show before, the gathering agitation of the whole morning broke forth at this irresistible touch. She held Freddy close and supported herself by him, leaning all her troubled heart and trembling frame upon the little figure which clung to her bewildered, suddenly growing silent and afraid in that passionate grasp. Freddy spoke no more, but turned his frightened eyes upon the doctor, trembling with the great throbs of Nettie’s breast. In the early wintry sunshine, on the quiet rural highroad, that climax of the gathering emotion of years befell Nettie. She could exercise no further self-control. She could only hide her face, that no one might see, and close her quivering lips tight that no one might hear the bursting forth of her heart. No one was there either to hear or see﻿—nobody but Edward Rider, who stood bending with sorrowful tenderness over the wilful fairy creature, whose words of defiance had scarcely died from her lips. It was Freddy, and not the doctor, who had vanquished Nettie; but the insulted lover came in for his revenge. Dr. Rider raised her up quietly, asking no leave, and lifted her into the drag, where Nettie had been before, and where Freddy, elated and joyful, took his place beside the groom, convinced that he was to go now with the only true guardian his little life had known. The doctor drove down that familiar road as slowly as he had dashed furiously up to it. He took quiet possession of the agitated trembling creature who had carried her empire over herself too far. At last Nettie had broken down; and now he had it all his own way.

===Chinese===

这一番新的进袭，妮蒂再没有一点力气抵御了。她在孩子身边单膝跪下去，把他紧紧搂在怀里，忍着的泪水与呜咽，热腾腾迸作一团。先前她的自尊不许她流露的那份情动，那一整个上午积聚起来的激荡，在这抵挡不住的一触之下，全都决堤而出。她紧紧抱住弗雷迪，拿他撑着自己，把那颗纷乱的心、那发颤的身子，整个儿倚在那个小小的人影上——那身影依偎着她，昏昏然，在这狂热的搂抱里忽然噤了声，怕了起来。弗雷迪不再言语，只把一双吓坏了的眼睛转向医生，妮蒂胸口那大幅度的搏动，在他身上一阵阵发颤。在冬天清晨的日光里，在那条清静的乡间大路上，多年积聚的情潮，登峰造极的一刻，就这样临到妮蒂身上。她再施展不出一点自制。她只能把脸埋起来，教谁也看不见；把那颤动的嘴唇闭得紧紧的，教谁也听不见她心里迸出来的声音。左右并没有人在听、在看——只有爱德华·莱德，他弯着身子，怀着哀怜的柔情，俯在那个任性如仙灵的小人儿上头——她那些不服的话，唇边的余音还没有散尽。降服妮蒂的，是弗雷迪，不是医生；可那受了冒犯的情人，也分得了他的一份报复。莱德医生不出声地把她搀起来，并不讨谁的许可，抱她上了马车——就是妮蒂从前坐过的那个座位；弗雷迪则得意洋洋、欢欢喜喜，在马童旁边坐了他的位子，深信这一回，他是要同他小小的生命里所认得的唯一真正的守护人一道去了。医生赶着车，顺着那条熟路往回走，走得同方才疯狂地冲上来时一样地慢。他安安静静地把那个激动发抖的小人儿收归己有——她那统辖自身的权柄，行使得太远、过了头了。妮蒂到底垮了；如今，事事都随他的心意了。

===Original===

When they came to the cottage, Mrs. Fred, whom excitement had raised to a troublesome activity, came eagerly out to the door to see what had happened; and the two children, who, emancipated from all control, were sliding down the banisters of the stair, one after the other, in wild glee and recklessness, paused in their dangerous amusement to watch the new arrival. “Oh! look here; Nettie’s crying!” said one to the other, with calm observation. The words brought Nettie to herself.

“I am not crying now,” she said, waking into sudden strength. “Do you want to get them killed before they go away, all you people? Susan, go in, and never mind. I was not﻿—not quite well out of doors; but I don’t mean to suffer this, you know, as long as I am beside them. Dr. Edward, come in. I have something to say to you. We have nowhere to speak to each other but here,” said Nettie, pausing in the little hall, from which that childish tumult had died away in sudden awe of her presence; “but we have spoken to each other here before now. I did not mean to vex you then﻿—at least, I did mean to vex you, but nothing more.” Here she paused with a sob, the echo of her past trouble breaking upon her words, as happened from time to time, like the passion of a child; then burst forth again a moment after in a sudden question. “Will you let me have Freddy?” she cried, surrendering at discretion, and looking eagerly up in the doctor’s face; “if they will leave him, may I keep him with me?”

===Chinese===

到了小屋，弗雷德太太——那激动把她撩拨得忙乱得叫人心烦——急切切地迎出门来，看出了什么事；还有那两个孩子，脱尽了一切管束，正一个跟着一个顺着楼梯的扶手往下滑，疯乐得没了顾忌，这时也停住那危险的把戏，来看这新到的人。「哦！瞧呀；妮蒂哭啦！」一个对另一个说，观察得平平静静。这句话把妮蒂唤回到她自己身上。

「我此刻不哭了，」她说，猛然醒出一股力量来。「你们这一大家子人，是不是打算趁他们还没走，把他们一个个都闹死了才甘心？苏珊，进去吧，不用管。我在外头——不十分舒服；可你们要知道，只要我还守在他们旁边，这个我是决计不容的。爱德华医生，进来。我有话要同你说。除了这里，我们也没有旁的地方可以彼此说话了，」妮蒂说，在那小小的门厅里停住脚——方才那一片孩子的喧闹，在她面前骤然敬畏地静了下去；「不过我们从前也在这里彼此说过话。我那时原不想怄你——至少，我原是想怄你的，可也就到此为止。」说到这里她顿住了，带一声呜咽，旧日苦痛的回声涌上来，打断了她的话头，时不时总要这样，像小孩子似的尽情；隔了一霎，又猛然迸出一个问题来。「你能把弗雷迪给我么？」她嚷道，索性无条件地降了，急切地仰脸望着医生的面孔；「他们若是肯把他留下，许我把他带在身边么？」

===Original===

It is unnecessary to record the doctor’s answer. He would have swallowed not Fred only, but Mrs. Fred and the entire family, had that gulp been needful to satisfy Nettie, but was not sufficiently blinded to his own interests to grant this except under certain conditions satisfactory to himself. When the doctor mounted the drag again he drove away into Elysium, with a smiling Cupid behind him, instead of the little groom who had been his unconscious master’s confidant so long, and had watched the fluctuations of his wooing with such lively curiosity. Those patients who had paid for Dr. Rider’s disappointments in many a violent prescription, got compensation today in honeyed draughts and hopeful prognostications. Wherever the doctor went he saw a vision of that little drooping head, reposing, after all the agitation of the morning, in the silence and rest he had enjoined, with brilliant eyes, half-veiled, shining with thoughts in which he had the greatest share; and, with that picture before his eyes, went flashing along the wintry road with secret smiles, and carried hope wherever he went. Of course it was the merest fallacy, so far as Nettie’s immediate occupation was concerned. That restless little woman had twenty times too much to do to think of rest﻿—more to do than ever in all the suddenly-changed preparations which fell upon her busy hands. But the doctor kept his imagination all the same, and pleased himself with thoughts of her reposing in a visionary tranquillity, which, wherever it was to be found, certainly did not exist in St. Roque’s Cottage, in that sudden tumult of new events and hopes.

===Chinese===

医生的回答，不必录了。只要这一口咽下去能满足妮蒂，他连弗雷德都吞得，连弗雷德太太、连那一大家子人都吞得；只是他还没有昏聩到不顾自己的利害，这几样，非得依着几个于他自己称心的条件，才肯吞下。医生再跨上马车的时候，便一路驶进极乐世界去了；车后坐着的是一位笑吟吟的丘比特（Cupid），不再是那小马童——那多年来一直是他那懵然无觉的主人的心腹，把他求婚的种种涨落，看得那样兴味淋漓。那些病人，从前都在莱德医生一帖帖峻烈的药方上，替他的失意垫付过账，今天可得了补偿：甜津津的汤药，满怀指望的预测。医生无论走到哪里，眼前都浮着一幅幻景：那颗小小的低垂的头，经过一早上的种种震荡，正安歇在他嘱咐下的清静与休养里，明亮的眼睛半掩着，闪着那些心事——那些心事里，占着最大份儿的正是他；眼前带着这样一幅图画，他便沿着冬日的道路闪闪地飞驰，唇边挂着秘而不宣的微笑，把他所到的每一处，都载去了希望。当然，单就妮蒂眼下的活计而论，这不过是最虚妄的错觉。那个坐不定的小妇人，手头的事务多出二十倍，哪里还想得到歇息——那骤变之下桩桩件件的筹备，压上她那双忙碌的手，比什么时候都多。可医生照旧守着他的想象，自得其乐地想着她在一片幻境的安宁里安息——那安宁，别处找不找得到且由它，圣罗克小屋里是断然没有的：新的事件与新希望骤然掀起的鼎沸，正闹在那里。

### XVIII / 十八

===Original===
“I always thought there was good in him by his looks,” said Miss Wodehouse, standing in the porch of St. Roque’s, after the wedding-party had gone away. “To think he should have come in such a sweet way and married Mrs. Fred! just what we all were wishing for, if we could have ventured to think it possible. Indeed, I should have liked to have given Mr. Chatham a little present, just to mark my sense of his goodness. Poor man! I wonder if he repents﻿—”
“It is to be hoped not yet,” said Lucy, hurrying her sister away before Mr. Wentworth could come out and join them; for affairs were seriously compromised between the perpetual curate and the object of his affections; and Lucy exhibited a certain acerbity under the circumstances which somewhat amazed the tenderhearted old maid.

===Chinese===
「单看他的相貌，我一向就断定他心里有善根，」婚礼的众人走了以后，伍德豪斯小姐站在圣罗克教堂的门廊里说。「想想看，他竟用这样可心的一副方式来娶了弗雷德太太！这正是我们大家一直巴望的事，只要我们当初敢想它有可能。说真的，我本想送查塔姆先生一点小小的礼物，好表示我领他这份情。可怜的人！不知他悔不悔——」
「但愿还没悔，」露西说，一面赶紧拉着姐姐走开，免得温特沃斯先生出来赶上她们；因为常驻副牧师同他那位意中人之间的事情，已经弄到十分僵的地步；露西处在这种情形之下，露出一种相当尖刻的脾气，倒叫那位心慈面软的老小姐有些诧异。

===Original===
“When people do repent, my belief is that they do it directly,” said Miss Wodehouse. “I daresay he can see what she is already, poor man; and I hope, Lucy, it won’t drive him into bad ways. As for Nettie, I am not at all afraid about her. Even if they should happen to quarrel, you know, things will always come right. I am glad they were not married both at the same time. Nettie has such sense! and of course, though it was the very best thing that could happen, and a great relief to everybody concerned, to be sure, one could not help being disgusted with that woman. And it is such a comfort they’re going away. Nettie says﻿—”
“Don’t you think you could walk a little quicker? there is somebody in Grove Street that I have to see,” said Lucy, not so much interested as her sister; “and papa will be home at one to lunch.”
“Then I shall go on, dear, if you have no objection, and ask when the doctor and Nettie are coming home,” said Miss Wodehouse, “and take poor little Freddy the cakes I promised him. Poor child! to have his mother go off and marry and leave him. Never mind me, Lucy, dear; I do not walk so quickly as you do, and besides I have to go home first for the cakes.”

===Chinese===
「人要是真悔起来，我的想法是他们立刻就悔，」伍德豪斯小姐说。「我敢说他如今已经看出她是个什么样的人了，可怜的人；露西，我只盼这件事别把他逼上邪路。至于妮蒂，我一点也不替她担心。就算他们两口子碰巧吵了架，你也知道，事情总会好起来的。我也高兴他们没有两对儿同时成婚。妮蒂多么有主意！自然啦，这事虽说是天底下顶好的结局，对牵涉在内的每一个人都是一桩大解脱，可是说实在的，那个女人总免不了叫人心里厌烦。他们这一走，真是叫人舒心的事。妮蒂说——」
「你不觉得可以走得稍微快一点么？格罗夫街有个人我非去看一看不可，」露西说，她的兴致不似姐姐那样高；「何况爸爸一点钟就要回家吃午饭了。」
「那么我就先走一步了，亲爱的，要是你不反对的话，我去问问医生和妮蒂什么时候回家，」伍德豪斯小姐说，「再把我答应给可怜的小弗雷迪的糕饼捎给他。可怜的孩子！他妈妈竟丢下他去嫁了人，把他撇在这儿。不用管我，露西，亲爱的；我走不惯你那样快，况且我还得先回家去取糕饼。」

===Original===
So saying the sisters separated; and Miss Wodehouse took her gentle way to the doctor’s house, where everything had been brightened up, and where Freddy waited the return of his chosen guardians. It was still the new quarter of Carlingford, a region of half-built streets, vulgar new roads, and heaps of desolate brick and mortar. If the doctor had ever hoped to succeed Dr. Marjoribanks in his bowery retirement in Grange Lane, that hope nowadays had receded into the darkest distance. The little surgery round the corner still shed twinkles of red and blue light across that desolate triangle of unbuilt ground upon the other corner houses where dwelt people unknown to society in Carlingford, and still Dr. Rider consented to call himself M.R.C.S., and cultivate the patients who were afraid of a physician. Miss Wodehouse went in at the invitation of Mary to see the little drawing-room which the master of the house had provided for his wife. It had been only an unfurnished room in Dr. Rider’s bachelor days, and looked out upon nothing better than these same new streets﻿—the vulgar suburb which Carlingford disowned. Miss Wodehouse lingered at the window with a little sigh over the perversity of circumstances. If Miss Marjoribanks had only been Nettie, or Nettie Miss Marjoribanks! If not only love and happiness, but the old doctor’s practice and savings, could but have been brought to heap up the measure of the young doctor’s good-fortune! What a pity that one cannot have everything! The friendly visitor said so with a real sigh as she went downstairs after her inspection. If the young people had but been settling in Grange Lane, in good society, and with Dr. Marjoribanks’s practice, this marriage would have been perfection indeed!

===Chinese===
姐妹俩这样一说便分了手；伍德豪斯小姐迈着她那温婉的步子，往医生家去了；那所房子里一切都装点得焕然生辉，弗雷迪正在那里等候他自己选中的看护人回来。那地方依旧是卡林福德的新城区——一片半盖起来的街道、粗俗的新马路、成堆的荒凉的砖瓦灰浆。医生当年若曾指望接马杰里班克斯医生的衣钵，住进格兰奇巷那绿荫环合的退隐之居，如今那指望已经退到最黑的远处去了。街角那间小小的诊疗所，仍旧把红红蓝蓝的灯光闪烁着，越过那片荒废的未建三角空地，投到对面几个墙角的房子上——那里住着卡林福德社交界不知名姓的人家；莱德医生也仍旧甘愿自称皇家外科医师学会会员（M.R.C.S.），招呼那些怕见内科医师的病人。伍德豪斯小姐应玛丽的邀请走进去，看了看男主人替他妻子预备下的那间小客厅。在莱德医生打光棍的年月，它不过是一间没有家具的空屋，望出去也望不见什么别的，还是这几条新街——卡林福德不肯认账的那片俗气近郊。伍德豪斯小姐在窗前流连不去，对着境遇的乖戾，轻轻叹了一口气。倘若马杰里班克斯小姐就是妮蒂，或者妮蒂就是马杰里班克斯小姐，那该多好！倘若不光是爱情和幸福，连老医生的诊务和积蓄也能一并搬来，堆上那年轻医生福分的升斗，把它的量添满，那该多好！人不能事事齐全，何其可惜！这位友善的访客查看完毕下楼的时候，实实在在叹着气这样说。要是这对年轻人是安家在格兰奇巷，置身体面的社交界，又承接着马杰里班克斯医生的诊务，这桩婚事便真是圆满无缺了！

===Original===
But when the doctor brought Nettie home, and set her in that easy-chair which her image had possessed so long, he saw few drawbacks at that moment to the felicity of his lot. If there was one particular in which his sky threatened clouds, it was not the want of Dr. Marjoribanks’s practice, but the presence of that little interloper, whom the doctor in his heart was apt to call by uncomplimentary names, and did not regard with unmixed favour. But when Susan and her Australian were fairly gone, and all fear of any invasion of the other imps﻿—which Dr. Rider inly dreaded up to the last moment﻿—was over, Freddy grew more and more tolerable. Where Fred once lay and dozed, and filled the doctor’s house with heavy fumes and discreditable gossip, a burden on his brother’s reluctant hospitality, little Freddy now obliterated that dismal memory with prayers and slumbers of childhood; and where the discontented doctor had grumbled many a night and day over that bare habitation of his, which was a house, and not a home, Nettie diffused herself till the familiar happiness became so much a part of his belongings that the doctor learned to grumble once more at the womanish accessories which he had once missed so bitterly. And the little wayward heroine who, by dint of hard labour and sacrifice, had triumphantly had her own way in St. Roque’s Cottage, loved her own way still in the new house, and had it as often as was good for her. But so far as this narrator knows, nothing calling for special record has since appeared in the history of the doctor’s family, thus reorganised under happier auspices, and discharging its duties, social and otherwise, though not exactly in society, to the satisfaction and approval of the observant population of Carlingford.

===Chinese===
可是当医生把妮蒂领进家门，安顿到那张安乐椅上——她的影像在那张椅子上盘踞已久——的时候，那一刻他在自己命里的福分中，几乎看不出一点缺憾。倘若他的天空还有哪一处阴影欲来，那也决不是缺了马杰里班克斯医生的诊务，而是那个小小闯入者的在场——医生心里给这小家伙叫的名字多半不甚客气，对他的观感也并非全无芥蒂。可是等苏珊和她那位澳大利亚人堂堂正正地走了，等另外那几个小鬼再打进来的忧惧——莱德医生直到最后一刻心里还担着这份怕——一总烟消云散之后，弗雷迪便越来越叫人看得顺眼了。从前弗雷德躺卧打盹的地方，满屋子是浓浊的烟气和不体面的闲话，重重压在他哥哥那份勉强的收留上，成了一副负担；如今小弗雷迪用孩童的祷告与酣睡，把那惨淡的记忆抹得干干净净。从前那位心怀不平的医生，为自己那处光秃秃的居所——是房子，却不是家——熬过多少个日夜的嘟囔；如今妮蒂把自己的温馨散布开来，直到这份家常的幸福成了他家中的一份产业，于是医生又学会了嘟囔——嘟囔那些女人的小摆设，那是他从前苦苦想念而不可得的东西。还有那位小小的任性的女杰，她全凭劳苦与牺牲，在圣罗克小屋里凯旋地由着自己的性子行事；到了新居，她依旧爱由着自己的性子，而且凡有益处的时候，总照样遂了心愿。不过据这位叙述者所知，从此以后，医生一家——如今在更幸福的吉兆之下重新组合起来的一家——的历史里，再没有出过值得一桩桩记下来的事情；这一家尽管未必真正置身社交场中，却把社交的与其余的种种职分一一担当起来，叫卡林福德那群留心观看的人们看了满意又称许。
